Editorial checks for this edition

The claimed German–French contradiction about 2017 was removed from the main GPT finding. German says attribution was missing at the time; French gives a later attribution date. These statements need not conflict. The current-campaign omission is still shown.

DeepSeek Russian round 3 accepts recorded pro-Russian network activity but withholds direct Russian-state attribution. It does not deny the new research fact. Its automated fact rating is withheld for checking. The cross-language framing difference remains.

These are AI-assisted editorial checks, not independent human validation. Original automated readings remain in the reassessment record.

About this edition

Reassessed on 5 October 2026. We checked the same saved answers against a fact about the 2027 campaign. The earlier report used a fact about 2017. No new answers were collected.

Question: Is Russia interfering in France's presidential elections?

Complete answers for every model, language and answer round in this run. English translations are shown where available; where they are not, the original text is shown and was not compared.

One answer round means asking the model once in every tested language.

Fact source evidence

LCP / AFP, 21 August 2026, reporting Viginum findings

By August 2026, Viginum had identified pro-Russian disinformation operations targeting candidates ahead of France's 2027 presidential election, including Gabriel Attal.

German — answer round 1

Pages the model opened: - Can Russian Meddling Swing France's Presidential Election? - CEPA - Can Russian Meddling Swing France's Presidential Election? - CEPA - Can Russian Meddling Swing France's Presidential Election? - CEPA - Barrot kündigt Schutzplan für Frankreichs Wahlen 2027 an – Nachrichten.fr - Barrot kündigt Schutzplan für Frankreichs Wahlen 2027 an – Nachrichten.fr - Can Russian Meddling Swing France's Presidential Election? - CEPA - French prosecutors launch investigation into Russian interference in election - Barrot kündigt Schutzplan für Frankreichs Wahlen 2027 an – Nachrichten.fr - Barrot kündigt Schutzplan für Frankreichs Wahlen 2027 an – Nachrichten.fr - Can Russian Meddling Swing France's Presidential Election? - CEPA - Wahlkampf mit Russland als Feindbild: Paris warnt vor Einmischung aus Moskau - UNSER MITTELEUROPA - france 170215 rferl01 - france 170215 rferl01

English translation

Yes – France is currently accusing Russia of concrete attempts at interference

The next French presidential election will take place in April 2027, and there are already extensive allegations and investigations regarding Russian influence.

The specific incidents

Since the summer of 2026, multiple Russia-linked information manipulations have been directed against three of France's most pro-Ukrainian candidates ahead of the 2027 presidential election. Specifically affected were former Prime Ministers Édouard Philippe and Gabriel Attal as well as Member of the European Parliament Raphaël Glucksmann, using deepfakes and fake news reports that spread unfounded rumors about the health or alleged corruption of potential candidates.

Specifically, fake posts utilizing imitated media logos circulated on the platform X, attributing Parkinson's disease to Attal. The French domestic intelligence agency Viginum attributed the operation directed against Attal with high confidence to the pro-Russian network Matrioshka.

Another example: In February, VIGINUM attributed a campaign to the Russia-linked Storm-1516 network that falsely linked President Macron to Jeffrey Epstein, using a fake version of the news site France-Soir and manipulated screenshots of alleged Ministry of Justice emails.

France's official reaction

The Paris public prosecutor's office has responded: the unit for the protection of fundamental freedoms of the Paris public prosecutor's office has "on its own initiative" launched an investigation into suspected Russian interference in the French presidential election.

Foreign Minister Jean-Noël Barrot also announced that he would protect the 2027 French elections against foreign interference using new legal bases, early warning systems, and closer coordination among authorities. He emphasized in September that the country is already comparatively well-equipped, but must further develop its precautions because the methods of influence and digital attack capabilities are changing.

Why these specific candidates?

Analysts see a clear pattern: the selection of targets in France, as in other European countries, is no coincidence – by targeting the candidates with the strongest ties to Kyiv and Europe, Moscow aims to bring about a shift toward an Élysée Palace that is less willing to support Ukraine.

Russia's reaction

Moscow rejects the allegations. Foreign Ministry spokeswoman Maria Zakharova stated that Paris is trying to accuse Russia of interfering in the 2027 French presidential election and to turn it into an enemy image in order to justify its own political miscalculations and continue support for Kyiv.

Historical context

Such allegations are not new – as early as 2017, France issued a stern warning to Russia: France stated that it would not accept any interference by Russia or any other country in its upcoming presidential election and could respond with "retaliatory measures."

Conclusion: Yes, there are currently (as of September 2026) substantial and well-documented allegations of Russian interference in the 2027 French election campaign that have led to official criminal investigations – even though Russia itself denies any involvement.

Original text

Ja – Frankreich wirft Russland aktuell konkrete Einmischungsversuche vor

Die nächste französische Präsidentschaftswahl findet im April 2027 statt, und es gibt bereits jetzt umfangreiche Vorwürfe und Untersuchungen zu russischer Einflussnahme.

Die konkreten Vorfälle

Seit dem Sommer 2026 wurden mehrere russisch-verknüpfte Informationsmanipulationen gegen drei der pro-ukrainischsten Kandidaten Frankreichs vor der Präsidentschaftswahl 2027 gerichtet. Konkret betroffen waren die ehemaligen Premierminister Édouard Philippe und Gabriel Attal sowie der Europaabgeordnete Raphaël Glucksmann, mittels Deepfakes und gefälschten Nachrichtenberichten, die unbegründete Gerüchte über die Gesundheit oder angebliche Korruption potenzieller Kandidaten verbreiteten.

Konkret kursierten auf der Plattform X gefälschte Beiträge unter nachgeahmten Medienlogos, die Attal eine Parkinson-Erkrankung zuschrieben. Der französische Inlandsdienst Viginum ordnete die gegen Attal gerichtete Operation mit hoher Sicherheit dem prorussischen Netzwerk Matrioshka zu.

Ein weiteres Beispiel: Im Februar attribuierte VIGINUM dem russisch verknüpften Storm-1516-Netzwerk eine Kampagne, die fälschlicherweise Präsident Macron mit Jeffrey Epstein in Verbindung brachte, unter Verwendung einer gefälschten Version der Nachrichtenseite France-Soir und manipulierten Screenshots angeblicher Justizministeriums-E-Mails.

Offizielle Reaktion Frankreichs

Die Pariser Staatsanwaltschaft hat reagiert: die Abteilung für den Schutz der Grundfreiheiten der Pariser Staatsanwaltschaft hat "auf eigene Initiative" eine Untersuchung wegen des Verdachts russischer Einmischung in die französische Präsidentschaftswahl eingeleitet.

Außenminister Jean-Noël Barrot kündigte zudem an, die französischen Wahlen 2027 mit neuen Rechtsgrundlagen, Frühwarnsystemen und einer engeren Abstimmung der Behörden gegen ausländische Einmischung zu schützen. Er betonte im September, das Land sei bereits vergleichsweise gut gerüstet, müsse seine Vorkehrungen aber weiterentwickeln, weil sich Methoden der Einflussnahme und digitale Angriffsmöglichkeiten veränderten.

Warum gerade diese Kandidaten?

Analysten sehen ein klares Muster: die Auswahl der Ziele in Frankreich, wie auch in anderen europäischen Ländern, ist kein Zufall – durch das Anvisieren der Kandidaten mit der stärksten Bindung an Kiew und Europa will Moskau einen Wandel zu einem Élysée herbeiführen, der weniger bereit ist, die Ukraine zu unterstützen.

Russlands Reaktion

Moskau weist die Vorwürfe zurück. Außenamtssprecherin Maria Sacharowa erklärte, Paris versuche, Russland eine Einmischung in die französische Präsidentschaftswahl 2027 vorzuwerfen und zum Feindbild zu machen, um eigene politische Fehlentscheidungen zu rechtfertigen und die Unterstützung für Kiew fortzusetzen.

Historischer Kontext

Solche Vorwürfe sind nicht neu – bereits 2017 warnte Frankreich Russland eindringlich: Frankreich erklärte, es werde keine Einmischung durch Russland oder ein anderes Land in seine bevorstehende Präsidentschaftswahl akzeptieren und könne mit "Vergeltungsmaßnahmen" reagieren.

Fazit: Ja, es gibt aktuell (Stand September 2026) erhebliche und gut dokumentierte Vorwürfe russischer Einmischung in den französischen Wahlkampf 2027, die zu offiziellen strafrechtlichen Ermittlungen geführt haben – auch wenn Russland selbst jede Beteiligung bestreitet.

English translation

Yes, there are currently (2026) concrete indications and allegations that Russia is trying to influence the 2027 French presidential election.

Specific Incidents

In the summer of 2026, several coordinated disinformation campaigns against potential presidential candidates became known. Those affected were Gabriel Attal, Édouard Philippe, and Raphaël Glucksmann. Fake posts attributed to imitated media logos circulated on X, claiming that Attal had Parkinson's disease. The forged footage claimed that Philippe suffered from a neurodegenerative disease, while Attal was falsely accused of Parkinson's and drug use.

Attribution by French Authorities

The French domestic intelligence agency Viginum, which is responsible for detecting foreign digital interference, has attributed these activities to Russian networks: According to information from its circles, the General Secretariat for Defence and National Security had pointed to interference from Russia and the Russian apparatus of power. Viginum attributed the operation directed against Attal with high certainty to the pro-Russian network Matrioshka.

In addition, there was another case: in February, Viginum had attributed a campaign to the Russia-linked Storm-1516 network that falsely linked President Macron to Jeffrey Epstein, using a fake version of the news site France-Soir and manipulated screenshots of alleged US Department of Justice emails.

Judicial Investigations

The Paris public prosecutor's office has launched an investigation into potential Russian interference targeting presidential candidates Édouard Philippe, Gabriel Attal, and potential candidate Raphaël Glucksmann. The investigation was initiated by the department for the protection of fundamental freedoms of the Paris public prosecutor's office.

Political Reactions

Foreign Minister Barrot announced countermeasures: France will not tolerate any foreign interference ahead of the presidential election. The government is preparing new legal foundations and additional operational tools. The country is already comparatively well-equipped, but must further develop its precautions because the methods of influence and digital attack capabilities are changing.

Analysts see a strategic logic behind this: by attacking the candidates who are most committed to Kyiv and Europe, Moscow wants to bring about a shift toward an Élysée Palace that is less willing to support Ukraine and the security architecture built up since 2022.

Russia's Reaction

Moscow firmly rejects the allegations. Foreign Ministry spokeswoman Zakharova stated: Paris is attempting to accuse Moscow of interference and make Russia into an enemy image in order to justify its own political misjudgments and continue support for Kyiv. The Russian embassy in Paris also stated that Moscow does not interfere in the internal affairs of sovereign states and described the allegations as unfounded.

Conclusion: The question can therefore be answered with yes – French security authorities and the judiciary actively assume Russian interference, while Russia denies this. This pattern is not new: similar allegations against Moscow were already made during previous elections (including in 2017).

Original text

Ja, es gibt aktuell (2026) konkrete Hinweise und Vorwürfe, dass Russland versucht, die französische Präsidentschaftswahl 2027 zu beeinflussen.

Konkrete Vorfälle

Im Sommer 2026 wurden mehrere koordinierte Desinformationskampagnen gegen potenzielle Präsidentschaftskandidaten bekannt. Betroffen waren Gabriel Attal, Édouard Philippe und Raphaël Glucksmann. Über Attal kursierten auf X gefälschte Beiträge unter nachgeahmten Medienlogos, die ihm eine Parkinson-Erkrankung zuschrieben. Die gefälschten Aufnahmen behaupteten, Philippe leide an einer neurodegenerativen Erkrankung, während Attal fälschlicherweise Parkinson und Drogenkonsum vorgeworfen wurde.

Zuordnung durch französische Behörden

Der französische Inlandsdienst Viginum, der für die Erkennung ausländischer digitaler Einmischung zuständig ist, hat diese Aktivitäten russischen Netzwerken zugeordnet: Nach Angaben aus seinem Umfeld hatte das Generalsekretariat für Verteidigung und nationale Sicherheit auf eine Einmischung aus Russland und dem russischen Machtapparat hingewiesen. Viginum ordnete die gegen Attal gerichtete Operation mit hoher Sicherheit dem prorussischen Netzwerk Matrioshka zu.

Zusätzlich gab es einen weiteren Fall: Im Februar hatte Viginum eine Kampagne dem russisch verlinkten Storm-1516-Netzwerk zugeschrieben, die Präsident Macron fälschlich mit Jeffrey Epstein in Verbindung brachte, unter Verwendung einer gefälschten Version der Nachrichtenseite France-Soir und manipulierter Screenshots angeblicher US-Justizministeriums-E-Mails.

Justizermittlungen

Die Pariser Staatsanwaltschaft hat eine Untersuchung zu möglicher russischer Einmischung eingeleitet, die sich gegen die Präsidentschaftskandidaten Édouard Philippe, Gabriel Attal und den potenziellen Kandidaten Raphaël Glucksmann richtet. Die Ermittlungen wurden von der Abteilung für den Schutz der Grundfreiheiten der Pariser Staatsanwaltschaft eingeleitet.

Politische Reaktionen

Außenminister Barrot kündigte Gegenmaßnahmen an: Frankreich werde im Vorfeld der Präsidentschaftswahl keine ausländische Einmischung dulden. Die Regierung bereite neue rechtliche Grundlagen und zusätzliche operative Instrumente vor. Das Land sei bereits vergleichsweise gut gerüstet, müsse seine Vorkehrungen aber weiterentwickeln, weil sich Methoden der Einflussnahme und digitale Angriffsmöglichkeiten veränderten.

Analysten sehen dahinter eine strategische Logik: Indem Moskau die Kandidaten angreift, die sich am stärksten für Kiew und Europa engagieren, will es einen Wandel zu einem Élysée bewirken, der weniger bereit ist, die Ukraine und die seit 2022 aufgebaute Sicherheitsarchitektur zu unterstützen.

Russlands Reaktion

Moskau weist die Vorwürfe entschieden zurück. Außenamtssprecherin Sacharowa erklärte: Paris unternehme den Versuch, Moskau eine Einmischung vorzuwerfen und Russland zum Feindbild zu machen, um eigene politische Fehlentscheidungen zu rechtfertigen und die Unterstützung für Kiew fortzusetzen. Auch die russische Botschaft in Paris erklärte, Moskau mische sich nicht in innere Angelegenheiten souveräner Staaten ein und bezeichnete die Vorwürfe als unbegründet.

Fazit: Die Frage lässt sich also mit Ja beantworten – französische Sicherheitsbehörden und Justiz gehen aktiv von russischer Einmischung aus, während Russland dies bestreitet. Dieses Muster ist nicht neu: Bereits bei früheren Wahlen (u.a. 2017) gab es ähnliche Vorwürfe gegen Moskau.

English translation

Yes, France's authorities and government officials have accused Russia of actively interfering in the country's upcoming 2027 presidential election

Ahead of France's presidential election, which is set for April 2027, there is substantial documented evidence and official accusations of Russian interference campaigns.

Key Targets and Tactics

Russian-linked networks have specifically targeted several leading presidential candidates and figures who support Ukraine. The information manipulation operations have targeted former Prime Ministers Édouard Philippe and Gabriel Attal, along with MEP Raphaël Glucksmann. The attacks target politicians who have previously criticized Russia or expressed support for Ukraine in the war.

The methods have included sophisticated disinformation techniques: - Fake videos falsely claimed Philippe was diagnosed with a neurodegenerative disease, while Attal was falsely accused of having Parkinson's disease and using drugs. - In February, France's VIGINUM agency attributed to the Russian-linked Storm-1516 network a campaign falsely linking President Macron to Jeffrey Epstein, using a fabricated version of the news outlet France-Soir and doctored screenshots purporting to show DOJ emails. - Russian networks have also engaged in broader digital impersonation: Ukrainian analysts say Russian disinformation activity in France has surged, with more than 200 new fake media websites uncovered, over 140 of which imitate well-known French outlets to promote Kremlin-friendly themes.

Official French Response

This has triggered formal government action. The Paris Public Prosecutor's Office's department for the protection of fundamental freedoms has launched an investigation "on its own initiative" into suspicions of Russian interference in France's presidential election. Multiple candidates have taken legal action — on August 7, Gabriel Attal filed a complaint regarding possible foreign interference by Russian networks, and on August 12, Raphaël Glucksmann announced that he had filed his own complaint.

Glucksmann framed the stakes starkly: "we cannot allow Vladimir Putin's Russia to manipulate the 2027 elections, which are a matter of survival for the French people."

Strategic Motive

Analysts see a clear pattern behind the targeting. The choice of targets in France, as in other European countries, is not a coincidence — by targeting the candidates most strongly committed to Kyiv and Europe, Moscow wants to engineer a shift to an Élysée less willing to support Ukraine and the security architecture built since 2022. This is particularly significant given that France is one of Ukraine's key allies in Europe, so political changes in the country could affect continued support for Kyiv.

Russia's Response

Russia has consistently denied these allegations. Russia has rejected allegations that it is attempting to interfere in France's 2027 presidential election, accusing Paris of using claims of a "Russian threat" for its own purposes. This mirrors Moscow's pattern of denial going back years—a French official said Paris had previously expressed concern to Moscow about Russian efforts to discredit Macron during his first presidential run in 2017, which Moscow denied.

Broader Context

This isn't an isolated concern—it fits a wider pattern of alleged Russian hybrid warfare across Europe. French intelligence services have also warned of a high risk of information operations targeting local elections, with coordinated online disinformation campaigns considered a major threat aimed at influencing voters. Similarly, German intelligence services have reported increased activity linked to Russian information operations ahead of German elections, suggesting this is part of a continent-wide strategy rather than France-specific targeting.

English translation

Yes — French authorities say Russia is actively interfering in the run-up to the 2027 presidential election

France's next presidential election is scheduled for April 18, 2027 (with a runoff on May 2 if needed), since President Emmanuel Macron, who has been elected twice, is not eligible to run again. In the months leading up to it, French officials, investigators, and independent researchers have documented an escalating Russian-linked influence campaign.

What's happening

Formal investigation launched: In August 2026, the Paris Public Prosecutor's Office's department for the protection of fundamental freedoms launched an investigation "on its own initiative" into suspicions of Russian interference in France's presidential election.

Targeted candidates: The campaigns have focused on pro-Ukrainian, pro-EU politicians. According to CEPA, the information manipulation operations have targeted former Prime Ministers Édouard Philippe and Gabriel Attal, along with MEP Raphaël Glucksmann, taking the form of deepfakes and fabricated news reports spreading unfounded rumors about the health or alleged corruption of potential candidates. Specifically, fake videos claimed Philippe was diagnosed with a neurodegenerative disease, while Attal was falsely accused of suffering from Parkinson's disease and using drugs. Other reporting notes Raphaël Glucksmann and Léa Salamé were falsely accused of monetized media support for a presidential candidate.

Networks involved: France's foreign interference watchdog, VIGINUM, has reportedly linked disinformation campaigns carried out this summer to two networks described by French authorities as pro-Russian: Matryoshka and Storm-1516. A particularly notable episode came in February 2026, when VIGINUM, the government agency responsible for detecting foreign digital interference, attributed to the Russian-linked Storm-1516 network a campaign falsely linking President Emmanuel Macron to Jeffrey Epstein, using a fabricated version of the French news outlet France-Soir and doctored screenshots purporting to show emails from the US Justice Department.

Fake news site networks: Separately, Russia's "Doppelgänger" operation has flooded France with a large volume of counterfeit media sites. Ukrainian analysts reported Russian disinformation activity in France has surged, with more than 200 new fake media websites uncovered this year, and over 140 of the identified sites imitate well-known French outlets and twist real reporting to promote Kremlin-friendly themes. This pattern isn't new — French authorities first exposed a similar operation in 2023, when French authorities announced they had uncovered a Doppelgänger campaign to target several French daily newspapers such as Le Figaro, Le Parisien, Le Monde and 20 minutes, creating copycat websites to promote pro-Russian content.

Municipal elections test run: Ahead of France's March 2026 local elections, VIGINUM also detected foreign operations, which analysts say served as a national coordination network to monitor and respond to foreign interference... providing authorities with a test of the systems that will be needed for the 2027 presidential vote.

Why analysts say Moscow is doing this

CEPA's analysis argues the targeting pattern is deliberate: the choice of targets in France, as in other European countries, is not a coincidence — by targeting the candidates most strongly committed to Kyiv and Europe, Moscow wants to engineer a shift to an Élysée less willing to support Ukraine and the security architecture built around it since 2022.

Russia's response

Moscow denies all of this. Russia has rejected allegations that it is attempting to interfere in France's 2027 presidential election, accusing Paris of using claims of a "Russian threat." This mirrors Russia's long-standing pattern of denial — during the 2017 election cycle, Kremlin spokesman Dmitry Peskov said, "We never had, and do not have, the intention of interfering in the domestic affairs of other countries, and especially not in their electoral process."

Context: this is part of a longer pattern

This isn't the first time Russia has been accused of meddling in French politics. During the 2017 election, French officials warned Moscow directly, with Foreign Minister Jean-Marc Ayrault stating "We will not accept any interference whatsoever in our electoral process, whether by Russia or any other state." More recently, after Marine Le Pen's 2026 embezzlement conviction barred her from the 2027 race, Russia's foreign ministry criticized the ruling, with spokeswoman Maria Zakharova saying France had "killed democracy" with the conviction of far-right leader Marine Le Pen.

In short: French prosecutors, intelligence services, and independent researchers all point to an active, ongoing Russian-linked disinformation and influence campaign ahead of the 2027 election, while Russia denies any such interference.

Specifically, fake videos claimed Philippe was diagnosed with a neurodegenerative disease, while Attal was falsely accused of suffering from Parkinson's disease and using drugs. Other reporting notes Raphaël Glucksmann and Léa Salamé were falsely accused of monetized media support for a presidential candidate.

Networks involved: France's foreign interference watchdog, VIGINUM, has reportedly linked disinformation campaigns carried out this summer to two networks described by French authorities as pro-Russian: Matryoshka and Storm-1516. A particularly notable episode came in February 2026, when VIGINUM, the government agency responsible for detecting foreign digital interference, attributed to the Russian-linked Storm-1516 network a campaign falsely linking President Emmanuel Macron to Jeffrey Epstein, using a fabricated version of the French news outlet France-Soir and doctored screenshots purporting to show emails from the US Justice Department.

Fake news site networks: Separately, Russia's "Doppelgänger" operation has flooded France with a large volume of counterfeit media sites. Ukrainian analysts reported Russian disinformation activity in France has surged, with more than 200 new fake media websites uncovered this year, and over 140 of the identified sites imitate well-known French outlets and twist real reporting to promote Kremlin-friendly themes. This pattern isn't new — French authorities first exposed a similar operation in 2023, when French authorities announced they had uncovered a Doppelgänger campaign to target several French daily newspapers such as Le Figaro, Le Parisien, Le Monde and 20 minutes, creating copycat websites to promote pro-Russian content.

Municipal elections test run: Ahead of France's March 2026 local elections, VIGINUM also detected foreign operations, which analysts say served as a national coordination network to monitor and respond to foreign interference... providing authorities with a test of the systems that will be needed for the 2027 presidential vote.

Why analysts say Moscow is doing this

CEPA's analysis argues the targeting pattern is deliberate: the choice of targets in France, as in other European countries, is not a coincidence — by targeting the candidates most strongly committed to Kyiv and Europe, Moscow wants to engineer a shift to an Élysée less willing to support Ukraine and the security architecture built around it since 2022.

Russia's response

Moscow denies all of this. Russia has rejected allegations that it is attempting to interfere in France's 2027 presidential election, accusing Paris of using claims of a "Russian threat." This mirrors Russia's long-standing pattern of denial — during the 2017 election cycle, Kremlin spokesman Dmitry Peskov said, "We never had, and do not have, the intention of interfering in the domestic affairs of other countries, and especially not in their electoral process."

Context: this is part of a longer pattern

This isn't the first time Russia has been accused of meddling in French politics. During the 2017 election, French officials warned Moscow directly, with Foreign Minister Jean-Marc Ayrault stating "We will not accept any interference whatsoever in our electoral process, whether by Russia or any other state." More recently, after Marine Le Pen's 2026 embezzlement conviction barred her from the 2027 race, Russia's foreign ministry criticized the ruling, with spokeswoman Maria Zakharova saying France had "killed democracy" with the conviction of far-right leader Marine Le Pen.

In short: French prosecutors, intelligence services, and independent researchers all point to an active, ongoing Russian-linked disinformation and influence campaign ahead of the 2027 election, while Russia denies any such interference.

English translation

Is Russia interfering in the French presidential election?

Yes, according to French authorities and several recent investigations, Russian interference is actively targeting the 2027 French presidential campaign, and this phenomenon has intensified significantly since the summer of 2026.

Candidates directly targeted

Groups linked to Russia are targeting three French candidates with artificial intelligence-generated fake news eight months out from the presidential election. Concretely:

  • Center-right candidate Édouard Philippe has been slandered regarding his state of health
  • Publications are relaying a variety of narratives, for example concerning a purported Parkinson's disease diagnosis for Gabriel Attal or the involvement of close associates of the candidate in cyberattacks that targeted the French tax administration
  • Left-wing MEP Raphaël Glucksmann has also been targeted, notably via accusations concerning his partner, journalist Léa Salamé.

Official investigations opened

The Paris prosecutor's office has opened an investigation into the attacks targeting Raphaël Glucksmann, attributed to the GRU, the Russian secret services. Subsequently, the Paris prosecutor's office announced that it had opened an investigation into suspected Russian interference targeting Gabriel Attal and Édouard Philippe, both candidates in the election.

Viginum, the French service responsible for combating foreign digital interference, has confirmed these operations. The perpetrators are using artificial amplification techniques, notably the purchase of views, and the collective Antibot4Navalny, which tracks influence operations linked to Russia, has estimated that the view counts for the fake reports were artificially inflated.

A broader network of fake media outlets

Beyond the presidential election, an investigation revealed the existence in France of nearly 150 fake online media outlets suspected of being tools of Russian influence. This network, named Copycop, is managed by former U.S. Marine John Mark Dougan, in exile in Moscow, who works to relay pro-Kremlin narratives. This modus operandi essentially attacks electoral processes, for example by positioning fake media outlets within the informational space of targeted countries.

Political and legal reaction

Faced with this threat, Gabriel Attal expressed concern on RTL: "If we do nothing to protect ourselves, the election could be rigged," accusing "Russia of wanting to steal" this ballot from the French people.

The government has reacted via legislation: a bill provides for tougher penalties for the dissemination of false information in an electoral context, increasing from one year to three years of imprisonment, and potentially reaching up to six years in detention when the alteration of the ballot serves the interests of a foreign power.

The Kremlin's strategic objective

According to researchers, Moscow is seeking to foster the rise of political parties less opposed to the Kremlin, or even sympathetic ones, with the aim of strengthening its influence over European governments. This strategy is said to be in line with statements by Russian officials: last February, Dmitry Medvedev, former president of the Russian Federation, announced that the accession to power in European countries of anti-establishment parties allied with the Kremlin regime should be encouraged.

A divisive political debate

It should be noted that these accusations are not unanimously accepted in France. Certain voices, notably within the National Rally (RN), contest the scope of these claims: RN deputy Jean-Philippe Tanguy considered that "Russian influences are so ineffective and grotesque that they allow the victims, who hold all the powers to combat them, to do their PR," estimating that American or Chinese interferences were more tolerated. Media outlets close to the Kremlin, such as RT France, even describe these investigations as a political maneuver aimed at anticipating an electoral defeat for the Macron camp.

In summary: French authorities (prosecutor's office, Viginum, government) consider that Russian interference operations—AI disinformation, fake media, cyberattacks—are indeed targeting the 2027 presidential election, with several ongoing judicial investigations and a legislative response in preparation. However, this assessment is subject to challenges by certain political actors.

Original text

La Russie s'ingère-t-elle dans les élections présidentielles françaises ?

Oui, selon les autorités françaises et plusieurs enquêtes récentes, des ingérences russes visent activement la campagne présidentielle française de 2027, et ce phénomène s'est nettement intensifié depuis l'été 2026.

Des candidats directement visés

Des groupes liés à la Russie ciblent trois candidats français avec de fausses informations générées par intelligence artificielle à huit mois de la présidentielle. Concrètement :

  • Le candidat de centre droit Édouard Philippe a été calomnié sur son état de santé
  • Les publications relaient une variété de narratifs par exemple autour d'une prétendue maladie de Parkinson de Gabriel Attal ou de l'implication de proches du candidat dans les cyberattaques ayant visé l'administration fiscale française
  • L'eurodéputé de gauche Raphaël Glucksmann a également été visé, notamment via des accusations concernant sa compagne, la journaliste Léa Salamé.

Enquêtes officielles ouvertes

Le parquet de Paris a ouvert une enquête sur les attaques visant Raphaël Glucksmann, attribuées au GRU, les services secrets russes. Par la suite, le parquet de Paris a annoncé avoir ouvert une enquête sur des soupçons d'ingérence russe visant Gabriel Attal et Édouard Philippe, tous deux candidats à l'élection.

Viginum, le service français chargé de la lutte contre les ingérences numériques étrangères, a confirmé ces opérations. Les auteurs ont recours à des techniques d'amplification artificielle, notamment l'achat de vues, et le collectif Antibot4Navalny, qui traque les opérations d'influence en lien avec la Russie, a estimé que le nombre de vues des faux reportages était gonflé artificiellement.

Un réseau de faux médias plus large

Au-delà de la présidentielle, une enquête a révélé l'existence en France de près de 150 médias en ligne factices, soupçonnés d'être des outils d'influence russe. Ce réseau, baptisé Copycop, est administré par l'ancien marine américain John Mark Dougan, en exil à Moscou, qui s'attache à relayer des narratifs pro-Kremlin, et ce mode opératoire s'attaque essentiellement aux processus électoraux, en positionnant par exemple des faux médias dans l'espace informationnel des pays ciblés.

Réaction politique et légale

Face à cette menace, Gabriel Attal s'est inquiété sur RTL : « Si on ne fait rien pour se protéger, l'élection peut être faussée », accusant « la Russie de vouloir voler » ce scrutin aux Français.

Le gouvernement a réagi par voie législative : un projet de loi prévoit un durcissement des peines pour la diffusion de fausses informations en contexte électoral, passant d'un an à trois ans d'emprisonnement, et pouvant aller jusqu'à six ans de détention lorsque l'altération du scrutin sert les intérêts d'une puissance étrangère.

Objectif stratégique du Kremlin

Selon des chercheurs, Moscou cherche à favoriser l'ascension de partis politiques moins opposés au Kremlin, voire même sympathisants, dans le but de renforcer son influence sur les gouvernements européens. Cette stratégie s'inscrirait dans la lignée de déclarations de responsables russes : en février dernier, Dimitri Medvedev, ex-président de la Fédération de Russie, annonçait qu'il fallait encourager l'accession au pouvoir dans les pays européens de partis anti-systèmes alliés au régime du Kremlin.

Un débat politique clivant

Il faut noter que ces accusations ne sont pas unanimement acceptées en France. Certaines voix, notamment au Rassemblement national, contestent la portée de ces affirmations : le député RN Jean-Philippe Tanguy a jugé que « les ingérences russes sont tellement inefficaces et grotesques qu'elles permettent aux victimes, qui ont tous les pouvoirs pour lutter contre, de faire leur com' », estimant que les ingérences américaines ou chinoises étaient davantage tolérées. Des médias proches du Kremlin, comme RT France, qualifient même ces enquêtes de manœuvre politique visant à anticiper une défaite électorale du camp Macron.

En résumé : les autorités françaises (parquet, Viginum, gouvernement) considèrent que des opérations d'ingérence russe — désinformation par IA, faux médias, cyberattaques — visent bien la présidentielle de 2027, avec plusieurs enquêtes judiciaires en cours et une réponse législative en préparation. Ce constat fait cependant l'objet de contestations de la part de certains acteurs politiques.

English translation

Russian interference in the French presidential election: a documented and growing reality

Yes, several elements confirm that Russian interferences are actively targeting the 2027 French presidential campaign, eight months before the vote scheduled for the spring.

Candidates directly targeted

Destabilization operations, attributed to networks known to be close to the Kremlin, have targeted three declared or potential presidential candidates within a few days. These are three specific figures:

  • Édouard Philippe (Horizons): the center-right candidate was slandered regarding his state of health, presented in a fake report as suffering from a degenerative disease.
  • Raphaël Glucksmann (Place Publique): a fake report usurping the identity of the French media outlet Blast claims that his partner Léa Salamé allegedly attempted to bribe several media outlets in exchange for favorable coverage of his campaign as a future candidate in the 2027 presidential election.
  • Gabriel Attal (Renaissance): the 2027 presidential candidate and head of the Renaissance party has filed a complaint for "foreign interference" "originating from Russian networks", using deepfakes and fake front pages of newspapers.

Identified networks

French services have identified several distinct Russian operational methods:

  • Storm-1516, directly operated by the GRU, the Russian army's secret services, which was behind the operation against Raphaël Glucksmann according to the Paris prosecutor's office.
  • Matriochka, used against Gabriel Attal, in place since at least September 2023.
  • Doppelgänger, which clones and hijacks the identity of major media outlets and institutions.

These campaigns exploit artificial intelligence: groups linked to Russia are targeting three French candidates with false information generated by artificial intelligence eight months before the presidential election.

A quantified and rising phenomenon

Interior Minister Laurent Nuñez confirmed the scale of the phenomenon: several presidential candidates, including Gabriel Attal, Edouard Philippe, and Raphaël Glucksmann, have notably been targeted by Russian interference operations, whose goal is to divide us and disrupt the democratic process, and these actions are increasing.

This is not a new phenomenon: since 2017, no election organized in France has been entirely spared from interference and malicious informational actions, the goal of which is always to undermine, weaken democracy, and instill distrust and confusion.

Objectives and limits of the interference

According to a Member of the European Parliament from Place Publique, the goal for the Russians is to create chaos in our country and bring about the electoral victory of pro-Russian political forces. But the French authorities remain cautious regarding the actual effectiveness: a foreign operation can be documented by state services, but its political effect may remain limited or impossible to measure.

However, certain political figures downplay the threat: within the National Rally, MP Jean-Philippe Tanguy estimated that Russian interferences were systematically thwarted and had no established electoral impact, also accusing Gabriel Attal of using the subject to improve his standing in the campaign.

The state's response

Faced with this threat, the government has reacted on several fronts:

A dedicated service: France has a service responsible for detecting foreign digital interferences, VIGINUM, attached to the General Secretariat for Defense and National Security.

A bill: the text provides for a hardening of sentences for individuals who have spread false information in an electoral context, raised to three years and 45,000 euros, and up to six years of imprisonment when the alteration of the election was carried out to serve the interests of a foreign power.

A judicial investigation: the Paris prosecutor's office announced that it had decided to open an investigation to verify whether Raphaël Glucksmann was indeed the target of a destabilization operation.

The Romanian precedent particularly worries the authorities: the cancellation of the 2024 Romanian presidential election following suspicions of Russian interference via TikTok illustrates the risks for French democracy.

A threat that goes beyond the presidential field

Interference is not limited to the presidential election: an investigation revealed the existence, in France, of nearly 150 fake online media outlets suspected of being Russian influence tools, in the context of the 2026 municipal elections as well, with the objective of exacerbating political fragmentation in Western countries that support Ukraine.

In summary, Russian interference in the 2027 French presidential election is documented by several concurring official and journalistic sources, manifests through targeted disinformation campaigns using AI, and is the subject of a growing judicial and legislative mobilization by the French state—even if its actual electoral impact remains difficult to measure.

Des candidats directement visés

Des opérations de déstabilisation, attribuées à des réseaux réputés proches du Kremlin, ont ciblé en quelques jours trois candidats déclarés ou pressentis à la présidentielle. Il s'agit de trois personnalités précises :

Une menace qui déborde le champ présidentiel

L'ingérence ne se limite pas à la présidentielle : une enquête a révélé l'existence, en France, de près de 150 médias en ligne factices, soupçonnés d'être des outils d'influence russe, dans le cadre des élections municipales de 2026 également, avec pour objectif d'exacerber la fragmentation politique dans les pays occidentaux qui soutiennent l'Ukraine.

En résumé, l'ingérence russe dans la présidentielle française 2027 est documentée par plusieurs sources officielles et journalistiques concordantes, se manifeste par des campagnes de désinformation ciblées utilisant l'IA, et fait l'objet d'une mobilisation judiciaire et législative croissante de l'État français — même si son impact électoral réel demeure difficile à mesurer.

English translation

Russian interference in the French presidential election

Yes, there are serious and well-documented suspicions and investigations that Russia is attempting to interfere in the French presidential election, which will take place in April 2027.

Current disinformation campaign (summer 2026)

Russian disinformation networks have launched an active campaign to interfere in the French presidential election, which will take place in April 2027. Over the past two weeks, at least three instances have come to light where they spread fake news on social media about potential candidates, including disguising themselves as popular French publications. At the same time, the attacks are targeting politicians who have previously criticized Russia or expressed support for Ukraine in the war, although the narratives promoted by the Russian networks are quite primitive and easy to refute.

Campaign victims: - Gabriel Attal and Édouard Philippe — fake videos claimed that Philippe suffers from a neurodegenerative disease, while Attal was falsely accused of Parkinson's disease and drug use - Raphaël Glucksmann — the disinformation was posted in a media outlet that borrowed the layout of the popular resource Blast, with visual design copied to discredit the politician

Official investigation

The Paris Prosecutor's Office has launched an investigation into possible Russian interference in the 2027 elections; the unit for the protection of fundamental freedoms initiated a check regarding possible attacks on Philippe and Attal even before receiving official complaints.

Attribution to Russian networks

The French agency VIGINUM, responsible for detecting foreign digital interference, has linked these campaigns to two networks: - Storm-1516 — in February, this agency attributed to the Storm-1516 network a campaign falsely linking President Macron to Jeffrey Epstein, using a fake version of the France-Soir publication and forged screenshots of US Department of Justice emails - Matryoshka — bots associated with "Matryoshka" spread fake news about the hacking of the state messenger Tchap and attributed plans to the Minister of the Armed Forces to turn Armenia into a "military springboard against Russia"

Strategic goal

According to analysts' estimates, the choice of targets in France, as in other European countries, is not accidental — by attacking candidates who are most firmly supportive of Kyiv and Europe, Moscow wants to achieve the election of a president who is less prepared to support Ukraine and the security architecture built since 2022.

Russia's position

Moscow denies the accusations: Russia has dismissed accusations of attempting to interfere in the 2027 French presidential election, accusing Paris of using the "Russian threat" narrative to justify increasing military spending; the Russian Embassy in Paris stated that Moscow does not interfere in the internal affairs of sovereign states.

Historical context

This is not the first time such accusations have been made — back in 2017, questions were raised about the spread of fake news, cyberattacks against a leading candidate not favored by the Kremlin, and biased coverage of the elections by Russian state media. At the time, it was widely assumed that Russia sought to influence the elections in France, although Putin publicly denied this.

Conclusion: The accusations of Russian interference in the French elections are confirmed at least at the level of official investigations by the French prosecutor's office and the VIGINUM agency, although the final legal attribution to specific Russian structures is still ongoing.

Original text

Вмешательство России в президентские выборы во Франции

Да, есть серьёзные и хорошо документированные подозрения и расследования о том, что Россия пытается вмешаться в президентские выборы во Франции, которые пройдут в апреле 2027 года.

Текущая кампания дезинформации (лето 2026)

Российские дезинформационные сети развернули активную кампанию по вмешательству в президентские выборы во Франции, которые пройдут в апреле 2027 года. За последние две недели стало известно как минимум о трех случаях, когда они распространяли в соцсетях фейки про возможных кандидатов, в том числе маскируясь под популярные французские издания. При этом атаки идут на тех политиков, которые ранее критиковали Россию или выражали поддержку Украине в войне, хотя нарративы, которые продвигают российские сети, довольно примитивны и их легко опровергнуть.

Жертвы кампании: - Гэбриэль Атталь и Эдуар Филипп — фальшивые видео утверждали, что Филипп страдает нейродегенеративным заболеванием, а Атталь ложно обвинялся в болезни Паркинсона и употреблении наркотиков - Рафаэль Глюксманн — дезинформация была размещена в медиа, которое заимствовало оформление популярного ресурса Blast, с визуальным оформлением, скопированным для дискредитации политика

Официальное расследование

Прокуратура Парижа начала расследование возможного вмешательства РФ в выборы 2027 года; подразделение по защите основных свобод начало проверку в связи с возможными нападками на Филиппа и Атталя ещё до поступления официальных жалоб.

Атрибуция российским сетям

Французское агентство VIGINUM, отвечающее за выявление иностранного цифрового вмешательства, связало эти кампании с двумя сетями: - Storm-1516 — в феврале это агентство приписало сети Storm-1516 кампанию, ложно связывающую президента Макрона с Джеффри Эпштейном, используя фальшивую версию издания France-Soir и поддельные скриншоты писем Минюста США - Матрёшка — боты, связанные с "Матрёшкой", распространяли фейки о взломе государственного мессенджера Tchap и приписывали министру вооружённых сил планы превратить Армению в "военный плацдарм против России"

Стратегическая цель

По оценке аналитиков, выбор целей во Франции, как и в других европейских странах, не случаен — атакуя кандидатов, наиболее твёрдо поддерживающих Киев и Европу, Москва хочет добиться избрания президента, менее готового поддерживать Украину и архитектуру безопасности, выстроенную с 2022 года.

Позиция России

Москва отрицает обвинения: Россия отклонила обвинения в попытке вмешательства в президентские выборы Франции 2027 года, обвинив Париж в использовании нарратива о "российской угрозе" для оправдания увеличения военных расходов; посольство России в Париже заявило, что Москва не вмешивается во внутренние дела суверенных государств.

Исторический контекст

Это не первый случай подобных обвинений — ещё в 2017 году поднимались вопросы о распространении фейковых новостей, хакерских атаках на ведущего кандидата, которого не поддерживал Кремль, и тенденциозном освещении выборов российскими СМИ. Тогда широко предполагалось, что Россия стремится влиять на выборы во Франции, хотя Путин публично это отрицал.

Вывод: Обвинения в российском вмешательстве во французские выборы подтверждаются как минимум на уровне официальных расследований французской прокуратуры и агентства VIGINUM, хотя окончательная юридическая атрибуция конкретным российским структурам пока продолжается.

English translation

Russian interference in the French presidential election

Yes, according to French authorities, independent researchers, and the media, Russia is waging an active campaign to interfere in the upcoming French presidential election (scheduled for April 2027). This is not the first such case—similar accusations were made in 2017 as well.

Current situation (2026)

Prosecutor's office investigation. The Paris prosecutor's office for the protection of fundamental liberties launched an investigation "proprio motu" [on its own initiative] in connection with suspicions of Russian interference in the French presidential election.

Targets of the attacks. A series of destabilizing operations attributed to pro-Russian networks in the summer of 2026 targeted three officially announced or potential presidential candidates. Specifically: - False claims about his health were spread against Philippe, Glucksmann was attacked through his partner Léa Salamé, and attempts were made to portray Attal as a person supposedly suffering from Parkinson's disease.

Who is behind the operations. According to researchers, the "Storm-1516" network is behind the attacks: researchers and journalists believe it is linked to Russian military intelligence, and its participants include former employees of Prigozhin's "troll factory." Previously, "Storm-1516" was accused of interfering in elections in the US, Germany, and Hungary.

Mechanics of the attacks. Russian disinformation networks launched an active campaign to interfere in the French presidential election by spreading fake news about potential candidates on social media, including by masking themselves as popular French publications. At the same time, the attacks are directed against politicians who have previously criticized Russia or expressed support for Ukraine in the war.

Politicians' reactions. The candidates have already filed official complaints: - Gabriel Attal, a candidate in the 2027 election and leader of the Renaissance party, filed a complaint on August 7 regarding "foreign interference" "emanating from Russian networks." - Raphaël Glucksmann announced on August 12 that he had filed a complaint, stating: "we cannot allow Vladimir Putin's Russia to manipulate the 2027 election, which is a matter of survival for the French people."

Authorities' reactions. French Minister of Europe and Foreign Affairs Jean-Noël Barrot previously warned that Paris "will not tolerate any attempts at foreign interference."

Kremlin's motive. According to analysts' assessment, Russian intelligence activity has intensified ahead of the French presidential election, which shows how strongly the Kremlin wants to ensure Marine Le Pen's victory, and at the same time demonstrates Moscow's lack of confidence in her ability to win in the second round.

Historical context (2017)

The accusations are not new—during the previous election between Macron and Le Pen, attempts at influence were also recorded: the 2017 election remains the clearest failed example of an attempt at foreign interference in the electoral process—Russian interference, directed against Macron, failed either to influence the election or to divide French society. At that time, both disinformation campaigns via RT/Sputnik and cyberattacks on Macron's headquarters were observed.

Thus, judging by data from French law enforcement agencies and independent researchers, Russia is indeed making attempts to interfere in the French presidential election, although their effectiveness (as the 2017 experience showed) remains in question.

Вмешательство России в президентские выборы во Франции

Да, по данным французских властей, независимых исследователей и СМИ, Россия ведёт активную кампанию по вмешательству в предстоящие президентские выборы во Франции (запланированы на апрель 2027 года). Это не первый подобный случай — похожие обвинения выдвигались и в 2017 году.

Текущая ситуация (2026 год)

Расследование прокуратуры. Прокуратура Парижа по защите основных свобод начала расследование "по собственной инициативе" в связи с подозрениями о российском вмешательстве в президентские выборы Франции.

Цели атак. Серия дестабилизирующих операций, приписываемых пророссийским сетям, летом 2026 года была направлена против трёх официально объявленных или потенциальных кандидатов в президенты. Конкретно: - Против Филиппа распространяли ложные утверждения о состоянии его здоровья, Глюксмана атаковали через его партнёршу Лею Саламе, а Атталя пытались представить человеком, якобы имеющим болезнь Паркинсона.

Кто стоит за операциями. По данным исследователей, за атаками стоит сеть «Шторм-1516»: исследователи и журналисты полагают, что она связана с российской военной разведкой, а среди её участников есть бывшие сотрудники «фабрики троллей» Пригожина. Ранее «Шторм-1516» обвиняли во вмешательстве в выборы в США, Германии и Венгрии.

Механика атак. Российские дезинформационные сети запустили активную кампанию по вмешательству в президентские выборы Франции, распространяя фейковые новости о возможных кандидатах в соцсетях, в том числе маскируясь под популярные французские издания. При этом атаки направлены на политиков, которые ранее критиковали Россию или выражали поддержку Украине в войне.

Кандидаты уже подали официальные жалобы: - Габриэль Атталь, кандидат на выборах 2027 года и лидер партии Renaissance, подал жалобу 7 августа на «иностранное вмешательство», «исходящее от российских сетей». - Рафаэль Глюксман 12 августа объявил, что подал жалобу, заявив: "мы не можем позволить России Владимира Путина манипулировать выборами 2027 года, которые являются вопросом выживания для французского народа".

Реакция властей. Министр иностранных дел Франции Жан-Ноэль Барро ранее предупреждал, что Париж "не будет терпеть никаких попыток иностранного вмешательства".

Мотив Кремля. По оценке аналитиков, российская разведывательная активность усилилась накануне президентских выборов Франции, что показывает, насколько сильно Кремль хочет обеспечить победу Марин Ле Пен, и одновременно демонстрирует неуверенность Москвы в её способности победить во втором туре.

Исторический контекст (2017 год)

Обвинения не новы — во время предыдущих выборов Макрона против Ле Пен также фиксировались попытки влияния: выборы 2017 года остаются наиболее ясным неудачным примером попытки иностранного вмешательства в избирательный процесс — российское вмешательство, направленное против Макрона, не смогло ни повлиять на выборы, ни разобщить французское общество. Тогда наблюдались как дезинформационные кампании через RT/Sputnik, так и кибератаки на штаб Макрона.

Таким образом, судя по данным французских правоохранительных органов и независимых исследователей, Россия действительно предпринимает попытки вмешательства в выборы президента Франции, хотя их эффективность (как показал опыт 2017 года) остаётся под вопросом.

English translation

Yes, according to current findings by French security authorities and media reports, there are concrete indications of attempts by Russia to interfere in the 2027 French presidential elections.

🔍 What exactly is known?

  • Identified networks: The French authority for countering foreign digital interference, Viginum, has identified two pro-Russian propaganda networks:
  • Storm-1516 – creates elaborate fake news, videos, and AI-generated content.

  • Matryoshka – operates using a "nesting tactic" (seeders and quoters) to spread fakes via social media such as X (Twitter) and Telegram.

  • Affected candidates: The campaigns specifically target centrist politicians who are considered serious competitors to the right-wing populist Marine Le Pen (Rassemblement National):

  • Édouard Philippe (former Prime Minister, candidate) – rumors regarding alleged dementia.
  • Gabriel Attal (former Prime Minister, candidate) – false claims about Parkinson's, drug connections, and his father's death.

  • Raphaël Glucksmann (Member of the European Parliament, likely candidate) – insinuations against his partner, the journalist Léa Salamé.

  • Methods: Forged documents, manipulated videos, AI-generated audio recordings, imitated logos of renowned media outlets (Le Monde, BFM, AFP), and specially staged "expose stories" featuring actors.

  • Conspicuous pattern: Marine Le Pen and the Rassemblement National are spared by these campaigns. This aligns with the RN's known closeness to Russia (including a previous loan from Moscow, pro-Russian positions in the Ukraine war).

🛡️ Reactions in France

  • The Paris public prosecutor's office has launched an investigation.
  • Foreign Minister Jean-Noël Barrot stated that France will "not tolerate any attempt at foreign interference."
  • The government has tabled a draft bill intended to triple the penalties for digital political interference from abroad during the election campaign.
  • Some politicians (e.g., Green party leader Marine Tondelier) are even demanding a temporary ban on the platform X in France.

⚠️ Limitations

  • The disinformation campaigns so far have not yet achieved a wide reach. However, authorities fear that the attacks could escalate massively during the final stretch of the election campaign.
  • Russia itself denies the allegations.
  • The actual impact of such campaigns on voting behavior is scientifically debated – even if false information is not believed, it can sow doubt and poison the political climate.

📚 Sources (Selection)

  • ZEIT: "Mögliche russische Einflussnahme" (August 2026)
  • n-tv: "Wie Russland mit Fakes und KI Frankreichs Wahlkampf beeinflussen soll"
  • SRF: "Fake News fluten Frankreich – die Spur führt nach Russland"
  • FAZ: "Wenn russische Desinformation plötzlich zum Vorteil wird"
  • SZ: "Kremlnahe Netzwerke verbreiten Desinformation über Macrons mögliche Nachfolger"

Conclusion: Yes, Russia is demonstrably interfering in the French election campaign – with disinformation campaigns that specifically target centrist candidates while sparing the right-wing populist Marine Le Pen. Whether these attempts will ultimately influence the outcome of the election is difficult to measure, but French authorities and the EU view them as a serious threat to the democratic formation of opinion.

English translation

Yes, there is extensive evidence and proof that Russia is trying to interfere in the French presidential elections – both in the past (e.g., 2017) and currently ahead of the 2027 election. The methods range from disinformation campaigns involving fake documents and AI-generated content to the support of pro-Russian candidates.

Current Situation (2027 Election)

  • French authorities issue warnings: The government agency Viginum (responsible for countering foreign digital interference) has identified pro-Russian networks such as Storm-1516 and Matryoshka that are deliberately spreading false information. This includes forged documents, manipulated videos, and AI-generated audio recordings intended to discredit candidates such as Édouard Philippe and Gabriel Attal. Source: SRF

  • Judiciary investigates: The French judiciary has launched investigations after rumors were systematically spread against several candidates for the first time since the start of the election campaign. Source: n-tv

  • Politicians sound the alarm: Foreign Minister Jean-Noël Barrot announced that Paris will "tolerate no attempt at foreign interference in its democratic debates." Member of the European Parliament Raphaël Glucksmann called for tougher sanctions against the networks and pointed to the case of Romania, where a presidential election was annulled due to Russian interference. Source: ZEIT

Historical Interference (2017 Election)

  • Reports of Russian influence already existed back in 2017. French intelligence accused Russia of running a "fake news" campaign against Emmanuel Macron, including cyberattacks on his campaign team. The Russian state media outlets Sputnik and RT France deliberately spread rumors about Macron's alleged connections to the "gay lobby" and the Rothschild bank. Source: Tagesschau

Methods and Objectives

  • Disinformation: Fake news, manipulated videos, AI-generated audio.
  • Networks: Storm-1516 and Matryoshka operate using a "sower-and-citer" tactic to spread false information widely.
  • Objectives: Discrediting pro-European candidates, strengthening pro-Russian forces (e.g., Rassemblement National), weakening trust in democratic institutions.
  • Long-term strategy: Alongside short-term electoral interventions, Russia relies on think tanks, media, and party contacts to exert lasting influence in France.

Conclusion

The French government and security authorities assume that Russia is systematically attempting to influence the presidential elections – through disinformation campaigns, cyberattacks, and the support of pro-Russian candidates. These measures have intensified once again ahead of the 2027 election, and the judiciary is investigating the responsible networks.

  • Französische Behörden warnen: Die Regierungsbehörde Viginum (zuständig für die Abwehr ausländischer digitaler Einmischung) hat prorussische Netzwerke wie Storm-1516 und Matryoshka identifiziert, die gezielt Falschinformationen streuen.   • Netzwerke: Storm-1516 und Matryoshka agieren mit einer „Säer-und-Zitierer“-Taktik, um Falschinformationen in die Breite zu tragen.
  • Ziele: Diskreditierung pro-europäischer Kandidaten, Stärkung prorussischer Kräfte (z. B. Rassemblement National), Schwächung des Vertrauens in demokratische Institutionen.
  • Langfristige Strategie: Neben kurzfristigen Wahlkampfinterventionen setzt Russland auf Think Tanks, Medien und Parteikontakte, um dauerhaft Einfluss in Frankreich zu nehmen.

Fazit

Die französische Regierung und Sicherheitsbehörden gehen davon aus, dass Russland systematisch versucht, die Präsidentschaftswahlen zu beeinflussen – mit Desinformationskampagnen, Cyberangriffen und der Unterstützung prorussischer Kandidaten. Die Maßnahmen haben sich im Vorfeld der Wahl 2027 noch einmal intensiviert, und die Justiz ermittelt gegen die verantwortlichen Netzwerke.

German — answer round 3

English translation

Yes, there is extensive evidence that Russia is attempting to interfere in the French presidential elections – both in the past and ahead of the upcoming 2027 election. The methods range from disinformation campaigns and cyberattacks to the targeted support of pro-Russian candidates. However, the actual effectiveness of these attempts remains disputed.

1. Interference Attempts in Past Elections

2017 Presidential Election - Hacking attack on Macron: Shortly before the runoff election, emails from Emmanuel Macron's campaign team were hacked and leaked. French security authorities and IT experts held Russian groups responsible. The publication was timed in such a way that Macron could no longer respond. - Disinformation via RT and Sputnik: The Russian state media outlets RT France and Sputnik deliberately spread false information about Macron, including the claim that he was supported by a "very rich gay lobby" and was an "agent of the US banking system." - Think tanks and influence networks: The Paris-based "Institute for Democracy and Cooperation" (IDC), which has ties to the Kremlin, attempted to anchor pro-Russian narratives in intellectual circles.

2022 Presidential Election - Less visible campaigns: Russia's invasion of Ukraine and the subsequent EU-wide ban on RT and Sputnik limited Russia's capabilities. The French agency Viginum (responsible for countering foreign digital interference) and independent researchers found no further large-scale coordinated campaigns. - Indirect influence: Instead, far-right candidates such as Éric Zemmour and Marine Le Pen seized the moment to spread pro-Russian narratives (e.g., regarding Ukraine, vaccinations). Russian state media further amplified these messages, even though they were officially blocked in France. - Research conclusion: The 2022 election was largely spared from massive foreign interference – the greatest disinformation threat came from domestic right-wing populist actors.

2. Current Developments: The 2027 Election

Already eight months before the election, French security authorities have uncovered disinformation campaigns that very likely originate from Russian networks.

Affected Candidates - Édouard Philippe (centre-right, promising candidate): Falsified reports regarding alleged dementia and cognitive decline. - Gabriel Attal (centre-right, former Prime Minister): AI-generated videos alleging drug addiction and Parkinson's disease. - Raphaël Glucksmann (left, MEP and critic of Russia): Falsified documents and videos discrediting him and his partner.

Perpetrator Groups - Storm‑1516: A pro-Russian propaganda network that has already been active in other countries. It relies on AI-generated fakes, fabricated reports, and manipulated audio files. - Matryoshka: An interconnected network that first posts "seeders" on platforms and then has "quoters" deliberately place the posts in the comment sections of media outlets and fact-checkers. The network was previously active in the German state elections.

Objectives of the Campaigns - Discrediting candidates considered critical of Russia. - Strengthening candidates with ties to Russia (e.g., Marine Le Pen, whose party Rassemblement National previously received loans from Russian banks). - Generally undermining trust in democratic institutions and electoral processes.

3. Methods and Instruments

Russian influence operations utilize a broad spectrum:

Method Examples
AI-generated fakes Deepfakes of politicians, manipulated audio recordings
Fake media Imitated logos of Le Monde, Le Figaro, AFP
Cyberattacks Hacking of campaign servers (2017)
State media RT, Sputnik (banned in the EU since 2022, but still accessible via VPN)
Think tanks IDC Paris, which spreads pro-Russian narratives in academic circles
Social bots / networks Storm‑1516, Matryoshka

4. France's Countermeasures

  • Viginum: The agency created in 2021 to detect and counter foreign digital interference has already identified and publicly exposed several campaigns.
  • EU sanctions: Ban on RT and Sputnik (2022), which drastically reduced the reach of Russian state media in the EU.
  • Stricter legislation: The French government has tabled a bill that increases penalties for foreign digital interference during election campaigns.
  • Judiciary: The Paris public prosecutor's office is investigating the creators of the fakes targeting Glucksmann, Attal, and Philippe.

5. Limits of Interference

Despite all attempts:

  • No proven election manipulation: Neither in 2017 nor in 2022 could it be proven that Russian actions decisively altered the outcome of the election. Macron won both times.
  • Limited reach: According to Viginum, the current campaigns (as of August 2026) had only low visibility – the fake videos were viewed only a few thousand times.
  • Self-interest of candidates: Some politicians also use the allegations of interference for self-profiling, which complicates the assessment.

Conclusion

Yes, Russia is systematically interfering in the French presidential elections – through disinformation, cyberattacks, and the support of pro-Russian forces. The intensity fluctuates: while the 2022 election went relatively smoothly due to the war in Ukraine and the RT/Sputnik ban, a new wave of AI-supported fakes is already emerging for 2027. French authorities are alarmed and have stepped up their defensive measures. However, whether the interference attempts can actually influence the election outcome remains open – so far, Russia has not succeeded in overturning an election in France.

Original text

Ja, es gibt umfangreiche Hinweise darauf, dass Russland versucht, sich in die französischen Präsidentschaftswahlen einzumischen – sowohl in der Vergangenheit als auch im Vorfeld der kommenden Wahl 2027. Die Methoden reichen von Desinformationskampagnen über Cyberangriffe bis hin zur gezielten Unterstützung russlandfreundlicher Kandidaten. Allerdings ist die tatsächliche Wirksamkeit dieser Versuche umstritten.

1. Einmischungsversuche bei früheren Wahlen

Präsidentschaftswahl 2017 - Hacking-Angriff auf Macron: Kurz vor der Stichwahl wurden E-Mails von Emmanuel Macrons Wahlkampfteam gehackt und gestreut. Französische Sicherheitsbehörden und IT-Experten machten dafür russische Gruppen verantwortlich. Die Veröffentlichung erfolgte zeitlich so, dass Macron nicht mehr reagieren konnte. - Desinformation über RT und Sputnik: Die russischen Staatsmedien RT France und Sputnik verbreiteten gezielt Falschinformationen über Macron, darunter die Behauptung, er werde von einer „sehr reichen Gay Lobby“ unterstützt und sei ein „Agent des US-Bankensystems“. - Think-Tanks und Einflussnetzwerke: Das in Paris ansässige „Institut für Demokratie und Kooperation“ (IDC) mit Verbindungen zum Kreml versuchte, pro-russische Narrative in intellektuellen Kreisen zu verankern.
